be the graph embedding along
f
.

5.1.
Basics on
b
-functions

Given a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
on which
f
acts bijectively
(equivalently,
\mathcal{M}\simeq j_{+}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})
),
we consider the
\mathcal{O}_{X}[s]
-module

\mathcal{M}[s]f^{s}:=\mathcal{M}\otimes_{k}k[s]f^{s},

where
k[s]f^{s}
is a free
k[s]
-module with basis the symbol
f^{s}
.

In fact,
\mathcal{M}[s]
is not just a module over
\mathcal{O}_{X}[s]
, but a left module over
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}=\mathcal{D}_{X}\langle s,t\rangle
.
Here
t
acts by the translation
s\mapsto s+1
, that is

P(s)uf^{s}\mapsto P(s+1)fuf^{s}.

Moreover, a derivation
D\in{\mathcal{D}er}_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{X})

commutes with the
k[s]
-action and acts on an element
uf^{s}
, with
u\in\mathcal{M}
,
by the “expected” rule

D\cdot uf^{s}=(Du)f^{s}+s\frac{D(f)}{f}uf^{s}.

It is a fundamental result, due to Bernstein and Kashiwara (see
[
Bernstein
, p. 25]
), that if
\mathcal{M}
is as above and
\mathcal{M}|_{U}
is holonomic, then
for every local section
u
of
\mathcal{M}
, there is a nonzero
b(s)\in k[s]
such that

(31)

b(s)uf^{s}\in\mathcal{D}_{X}[s]\cdot(fu)f^{s}.

It is clear that the set of such
b(s)
is an ideal in
k[s]
; its monic generator is the
b
-function

b_{u}(s)
.

Remark 5.1

(Specialization to integral powers of
f
)
.

In the above setting, we may specialize
s
to integers. More precisely, for every

m\in{\mathbf{Z}}
, we have a morphism
of sheaves of rings
\alpha_{m}\colon\mathcal{D}_{X}[s]\to\mathcal{D}_{X}
that maps
P(s)
to
P(m)
for
P(s)\in\mathcal{D}_{X}[s]
and a surjective morphism of sheaves of

\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules
\beta_{m}\colon\mathcal{M}[s]f^{s}\to\mathcal{M}
where
\beta_{m}\big(Q(s)uf^{s}\big)=Q(m)f^{m}u
for every
Q(s)\in k[s]
and
u\in\mathcal{M}

(we here consider
\mathcal{M}
as a
\mathcal{D}_{X}[s]
-module via
\alpha_{m}
). In particular, by specializing
s
to
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}
in (
31
), we conclude
that
b(m)f^{m}u\in\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot f^{m+1}u
.

Example 5.2

(The Bernstein-Sato polynomial)
.

In the special case when
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{O}_{X}[1/f]
and
u=1
, the
b
-function
b_{u}(s)
is denoted by
b_{f}(s)
and it is called the

Bernstein-Sato polynomial
of
f
. It is an important result of Kashiwara
[
Kashiwara
]
that all roots of
b_{f}(s)
are negative rational numbers.
For a more precise statement regarding the roots of
b_{f}(s)
and some related
b
-functions, see Theorem
5.23
below.

Remark 5.3

(
b
-functions give coherence of push-forward)
.

As we have mentioned in Section
2.5
,
the existence of
b
-functions is a key ingredient in proving that if
\mathcal{N}
is a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{U}
-module, then
j_{+}(\mathcal{N})
is a holonomic

\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module. The starting point is showing that
j_{+}(\mathcal{N})
is coherent. To see this, suppose that
X
is affine
and
\mathcal{N}
is generated over
\mathcal{D}_{X}
by the global sections
u_{1},\ldots,u_{r}
. If
N\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
is such that
no
b_{u_{i}}(s)
has integer roots
<-N
, then by specializing
s
to
-j
in the definition of
b_{u_{i}}
, for
j\geq N
, we see that
\tfrac{1}{f^{j+1}}u_{i}\in{\mathcal{D}_{X}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{f^{j}}u_{i}
.
Therefore
j_{+}(\mathcal{N})
is generated over
\mathcal{D}_{X}
by
\tfrac{1}{f^{N}}u_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq r
.

Proposition 5.4
.

If
\mathcal{M}
is a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module on which
f
acts bijectively, then we have
an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{X}\langle s,t\rangle
-modules

(32)

\tau\colon\mathcal{M}[s]f^{s}\overset{\sim}{\to}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M}),\quad P(s)uf^{s}\mapsto P(-\partial_{t}t)(u\otimes\delta).

Moreover, the action of
\partial_{t}
on the right-hand side corresponds to the action of
-st^{-1}
on the left-hand side.

Proof.

Let us check the compatibility of
\tau
with the
\mathcal{D}_{X}\langle s,t\rangle
-action. It is clear that
\tau
is
\mathcal{O}_{X}[s]
-linear, hence we
only need to check the compatibility with the action of
{\mathcal{D}er}_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
and that of
t
.
Note that

\tau\big(t\cdot P(s)uf^{s})=\tau\big(P(s+1)fuf^{s}\big)=P(-\partial_{t}t+1)fu\otimes\delta

=P(-\partial_{t}t+1)tu\otimes\delta=tP(-\partial_{t}t)u\otimes\delta=t\cdot\tau\big(P(s)uf^{s}\big),

where the second to last equality follows from Lemma
3.2
i).

Suppose now that
D\in{\mathcal{D}er}_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
. We have

\tau\big(D\cdot P(s)uf^{s})=\tau\big(P(s)Duf^{s}+sP(s)\tfrac{D(f)}{f}uf^{s}\big)=P(-\partial_{t}t)Du\otimes\delta-P(-\partial_{t}t)\partial_{t}t\tfrac{D(f)}{f}u\otimes\delta

=P(-\partial_{t}t)Du\otimes\delta-P(-\partial_{t}t)D(f)u\otimes\partial_{t}\delta,\quad\text{while}

D\cdot\tau\big(P(s)uf^{s}\big)=D\cdot P(-\partial_{t}t)u\otimes\delta=P(-\partial_{t}t)\big(Du\otimes\delta-D(f)u\otimes\partial_{t}\delta\big),

hence
\tau\big(D\cdot P(s)uf^{s})=D\cdot\tau\big(P(s)uf^{s}\big)
.

Note next that
it follows from the second formula in Lemma
3.2
ii) that

(-1)^{m}\tau\big(s(s-1)\cdots(s-m+1)uf^{s}\big)=f^{m}u\otimes\partial_{t}^{m}\delta\quad\text{for all}\quad m.

Since
k[s]
has a basis given by
\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}(s-j)
, for
m\geq 0
, and since multiplication by
f
is invertible on
\mathcal{M}
,
it follows that
\tau
is a bijective map. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

We next extend slightly the notion of
b
-function, as follows. This more general notion will feature in the characterization of the
V
-filtration
in Corollary
5.15
.

Definition 5.5
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module.
We say that a local section
w\in\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})

has a
b
-function
if there is a nonzero
b(s)\in k[s]

such that
b(s)w\in V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w
. In this case, the set of such polynomials
b(s)
is a nonzero ideal of
k[s]
.
Its monic generator is the
b
-function

b_{w}(s)
.

Remark 5.6

(Compatibility of the two definitions of
b
-functions)
.

Note that if
\mathcal{M}
is a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module on which
f
acts bijectively,
u
is a local section of
\mathcal{M}
, and
w=u\otimes\delta
is the corresponding
section of
\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
, then

w
corresponds to
uf^{s}
via the isomorphism
\tau
in Proposition
5.4
and in this case
b_{w}(s)
is the monic polynomial of minimal degree
such that
b_{w}(s)uf^{s}\in\mathcal{D}_{X}[s]\cdot fuf^{s}
(hence
b_{w}=b_{u}
).
Indeed, this follows from the fact that
V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}=\mathcal{D}_{X}\langle s,t\rangle\cdot t
, hence
V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot(u\otimes\delta)=\mathcal{D}_{X}[s]\cdot(fu\otimes\delta)
.
In particular, by taking
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{O}_{X}[1/f]
, we see that
b_{\delta}(s)=b_{f}(s)
.

Remark 5.7

(Interpretation of
b_{w}
)
.

With the notation in Definition
5.5
, we note that in fact
b_{w}(s)
satisfies
b_{w}(s)\cdot\tfrac{V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w}{V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w}=0
.
This is due to the fact that
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}=\mathcal{D}_{X}[s]+V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
and
b_{w}(s)
commutes with the elements of
\mathcal{D}_{X}[s]
.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{N}=V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w
, then
b_{w}
is the minimal polynomial of the action of
s
on
\mathcal{N}/t\mathcal{N}
. This follows from the fact that

V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w=\big(t\cdot\mathcal{D}_{X}\langle s,t\rangle\big)\cdot w=t\mathcal{N}
.

Remark 5.8

(Behavior of
b_{w}
with respect to an open cover)
.

Suppose that
X=U_{1}\cup\ldots\cup U_{r}
is an open cover,
\mathcal{M}
is a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module, and
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
is nonzero. If
f_{j}=f|_{U_{j}}
and
\iota_{f}\colon X\hookrightarrow X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}
and
\iota_{f_{j}}\colon U_{j}\hookrightarrow U_{j}\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}
are the corresponding graph embeddings, then we have canonical isomorphisms

\iota_{f_{j}}(\mathcal{M}|_{U_{j}})\simeq\iota_{f}(\mathcal{M})|_{U_{j}}.

For
w\in\Gamma\big(X,\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})\big)
, set
w_{j}=w|_{U_{j}}
,
then it follows from definition that

b_{w}={\rm lcm}\big\{b_{w_{j}}\mid 1\leq j\leq r\}

(this means that
b_{w}
exists if and only if each
b_{w_{j}}
exists, and if this is the case, then we have the stated equality).

Remark 5.9

(Behavior of
b_{w}
when rescaling
w
)
.

Note that if
w\in\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
and
p\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
is an invertible function, then
b_{w}
exists if and only if
b_{pw}
exists and, if this is the case, then

b_{w}=b_{pw}
. Indeed, if
b(s)w=Q\cdot w
, for some
Q\in V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
, then

b(s)pw=(pQ)\cdot w=(pQp^{-1})pw

and
pQp^{-1}\in V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
. This implies that if
b_{w}
exists, then
b_{pw}
exists and
b_{pw}
divides
b_{w}
.
The converse follows by symmetry since
p
is invertible.

Remark 5.10

(Behavior of
V
-filtration and
b
-functions when rescaling
f
)
.

Given a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
, it is easy to compare the
V
-filtrations of
\mathcal{M}
with respect to
f
and
g=pf
, where
p\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
is an invertible function.
Note that if
\iota_{f}
and
\iota_{g}
are the graph embeddings corresponding to
f
and
g
, respectively, then
\iota_{g}=\varphi\circ\iota_{f}
, where

\varphi\colon X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}\to X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}
is the isomorphism given by
\varphi(x,t)=\big(x,p(x)t\big)
, so that
t\circ\varphi=pt
. We thus get an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
-modules

\varphi^{*}(\iota_{g})_{+}(\mathcal{M})\simeq(\iota_{f})_{+}(\mathcal{M}),

which in turn induces an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

\tau\colon(\iota_{g})_{+}(\mathcal{M})\to(\iota_{f})_{+}(\mathcal{M}),\,\,\tau\big(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tfrac{u_{j}}{(g-t)^{j}}\big)=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tfrac{p^{-j}u_{j}}{(f-t)^{j}}.

This has the property that
\tau(Pw)=\tau_{0}(P)w
, where
\tau_{0}\colon\mathcal{D}_{X}\langle t,\partial_{t}\rangle\to\mathcal{D}_{X}\langle t,\partial_{t}\rangle
is the isomorphism
of sheaves of rings which is the identity on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
and satisfies

(33)

\tau_{0}(t)=pt,\,\,\tau_{0}(\partial_{t})=p^{-1}\partial_{t},\,\,\text{and}\quad\tau_{0}(Q)=Q-Q(p)p^{-1}t\partial_{t}

for
Q\in{\rm Der}_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
(note that, in particular,
\tau_{0}(s)=s
).
By the last assertion in Remark
3.8
, the
V
-filtrations
on
(\iota_{f})_{+}(\mathcal{M})
with respect to
t
and
pt
coincide, hence
\mathcal{M}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
if and only if it has a
V
-filtration with respect to
g
,
and in this case we have

\tau\big(V^{\alpha}(\iota_{g})_{+}(\mathcal{M})\big)=V^{\alpha}(\iota_{f})_{+}(\mathcal{M})\quad\text{for all}\quad\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}}.

Moreover, since
\tau
is compatible with the action of
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{D}_{X}\langle t,\partial_{t}\rangle

(this follows from the definition of
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
in terms of the ideal of
X\times\{0\}
, but can be deduced also from the
explicit formulas (
33
)),
it follows from the definition that

b_{w}=b_{\tau(w)}
for every
w\in(\iota_{g})_{+}(\mathcal{M})
, in the sense that one exists if and only if the other one exists and in this case they are equal.
For example, we see that for every
u\in\mathcal{M}
and
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
,
if
v=u\otimes\partial_{t}^{m}\delta\in(\iota_{g})_{+}(\mathcal{M})
and
w=u\otimes\partial_{t}^{m}\delta\in(\iota_{f})_{+}(\mathcal{M})
, then

b_{w}=b_{\tau(w)}=b_{p^{-m-1}v}=b_{v},

where the last equality follows from Remark
5.9
.

5.2.
Existence of the
V
-filtration via
b
-functions

The following result provides the criterion for the existence of
V
-filtrations in terms of
b
-functions. We give the proof following an approach due to Sabbah.

Theorem 5.11
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module.

If
\mathcal{M}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
, then every section
w\in\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
has a
b
-function. Moreover, if
w\in V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
, then
all roots of
b_{w}(s)
are rational numbers
\gamma\leq-\alpha
such that
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{-\gamma}(\iota_{+}\mathcal{M})\neq 0
.

Conversely, if
w_{1},\ldots,w_{r}\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M})
generate
\mathcal{M}
as a
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module and if each
w_{i}\otimes\delta\in\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})

has a
b
-function whose roots are all rational, then
\mathcal{M}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
.

Before giving the proof, it is convenient to introduce a version of
V
-filtration indexed by integers. We do this in the setting of a smooth hypersurface (as usual, we will use it on
X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}
).

Definition 5.12
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module and
t\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
nonzero that defines a smooth hypersurface. A
pre-
V
-filtration
on
\mathcal{M}

(with respect to
t
) is a decreasing, exhaustive filtration
W^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}=(W^{i}\mathcal{M})_{i\in{\mathbf{Z}}}
on
\mathcal{M}

by quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, that satisfies the following properties:

a)

We have
V^{i}\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot W^{j}\mathcal{M}\subseteq W^{i+j}\mathcal{M}
for all
i,j\in{\mathbf{Z}}
.

b)

W^{i}\mathcal{M}
is locally finitely generated over
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
for every
i\in{\mathbf{Z}}
.

c)

We have
W^{i+1}\mathcal{M}=t\cdot W^{i}\mathcal{M}
for
i\gg 0
.

d)

There is a polynomial
p=p_{W}\in{\mathbf{Q}}[x]
, with all roots in
{\mathbf{Q}}
, such that
p(\partial_{t}t-i)\cdot W^{i}\mathcal{M}\subseteq W^{i+1}\mathcal{M}
for all
i\in{\mathbf{Z}}
.

If the polynomial
p_{W}
in d) has all its roots in
[0,1)
, then we call
W^{\bullet}
a
{\mathbf{Z}}
-indexed
V
-filtration
.
As before, if
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
is nonzero and
\iota=\iota_{f}\colon X\to X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}
is the corresponding closed immersion, we consider
pre-
V
-filtrations and
{\mathbf{Z}}
-indexed
V
-filtrations
on
\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
with respect to
t
.

The following proposition relates the various notions of
V
-filtrations.

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module and
t\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
nonzero that defines a smooth hypersurface.

i)

If
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
is a
V
-filtration on
\mathcal{M}
with respect to
t
, then
(V^{i}\mathcal{M})_{i\in{\mathbf{Z}}}
is a
{\mathbf{Z}}
-indexed
V
-filtration with respect to
t
.

ii)

If
\mathcal{M}
has a pre-
V
-filtration with respect to
t
, then it also has a
{\mathbf{Z}}
-indexed
V
-filtration.

iii)

If
(W^{i}\mathcal{M})_{i\in{\mathbf{Z}}}
is a
{\mathbf{Z}}
-indexed
V
-filtration on
\mathcal{M}
with respect to
t
, then
\mathcal{M}
has a
V
-filtration
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}

with respect to
t
and
W^{i}\mathcal{M}=V^{i}\mathcal{M}
for all
i\in{\mathbf{Z}}
.

Proof.

For the assertion in i), we only need to prove that property d) holds for some polynomial
p
with roots in
[0,1)
. Since
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
is discrete and left-continuous, it follows from
property ii) in Definition
3.5
that there are polynomials
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
with roots in
[0,1)
such that
p_{1}(\partial_{t}t)\cdot V^{0}\mathcal{M}\subseteq V^{1}\mathcal{M}

and
p_{2}(\partial_{t}t-1)\cdot V^{1}\mathcal{M}\subseteq V^{2}\mathcal{M}
. Since
V^{m+1}\mathcal{M}=t\cdot V^{m}\mathcal{M}
and
V^{-m}\mathcal{M}=\sum_{i=0}^{m}\partial_{t}^{i}\cdot V^{0}\mathcal{M}
for all
m\geq 1

(see assertion iii) in Corollary
3.12
), using Lemma
3.2
it follows easily that if
p=p_{1}p_{2}
, then
p(\partial_{t}t-m)\cdot V^{m}\mathcal{M}\subseteq V^{m+1}\mathcal{M}
for all
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}
.

Let us prove ii).
Note that if
W^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
is a pre-
V
-filtration on
\mathcal{M}
with polynomial
p_{W}
and for an integer
q
we put
\widetilde{W}^{m}\mathcal{M}=W^{m+q}\mathcal{M}
, then

\widetilde{W}^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
is again a pre-
V
-filtration with corresponding polynomial
p_{\widetilde{W}}(x)=p_{W}(x-q)
. By taking a suitable
q
(small enough) and replacing
W^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}

by
\widetilde{W}^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
, we see that we may assume that all roots of
p_{W}
are
<1
.

Suppose now that
\lambda
is a root of
p_{W}

and let us write
p_{W}=(x-\lambda)^{d}q(x)
, where
q(\lambda)\neq 0
. We define a new filtration
U^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
by the formula

U^{m}\mathcal{M}:=W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}+(\partial_{t}t-m-\lambda)^{d}\cdot W^{m}\mathcal{M}\quad\text{for all}\quad m\in{\mathbf{Z}}.

It is clear that this is a decreasing, exhaustive filtration and it is an easy exercise to see, using Lemma
3.2
, that since
W^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
satisfies conditions a), b), and c)
in Definition
5.12
, so does

U^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
. Let us show that we may take
p_{U}(x)=(x-\lambda-1)^{d}q(x)
. Indeed, for every
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}
, we have

(\partial_{t}t-\lambda-m-1)^{d}q(\partial_{t}t-m)\cdot W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}\subseteq q(\partial_{t}t-m)\cdot U^{m+1}\mathcal{M}\subseteq U^{m+1}\mathcal{M}

by definition of
U^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
and

(\partial_{t}t-\lambda-m-1)^{d}q(\partial_{t}t-m)\cdot(\partial_{t}t-m-\lambda)^{d}\cdot W^{m}\mathcal{M}

=(\partial_{t}t-\lambda-m-1)^{d}p_{W}(\partial_{t}t-m)\cdot W^{m}\mathcal{M}\subseteq(\partial_{t}t-\lambda-m-1)^{d}\cdot W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}

\subseteq U^{m+1}\mathcal{M}.

We thus conclude that
U^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
is a pre-
V
-filtration and we may take
p_{U}(x)=(x-\lambda-1)^{d}q(x)
. After applying this construction finitely many times, we may replace

\lambda

by a root in
[0,1)
, and after repeating the same process for the other roots of
p_{W}
, and replacing
W^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
by the final pre-
V
-filtration, we see that we may assume that all
roots of
p_{W}
lie in
[0,1)
, hence this is a
{\mathbf{Z}}
-indexed
V
-filtration.

We now prove iii). Let
\alpha_{1}<\ldots<\alpha_{d}
be the distinct roots of
p_{W}(x)
, which by assumption lie in the interval
[0,1)
.
For every
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}
and every
i
, with
1\leq i\leq d
, let
P^{(m)}_{i}
, with
W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}\subseteq P^{(m)}_{i}\subseteq W^{m}\mathcal{M}
be such that
P^{(m)}_{i}/W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}
is the generalized eigenspace with eigenvalue

\alpha_{i}+m
for the action
of
\partial_{t}t
on
W^{m}\mathcal{M}/W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}
. It is a standard linear algebra result that
we have
W^{m}\mathcal{M}/W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}P^{(m)}_{i}/W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}
. Since
\partial_{t}t=-s
is a
\mathcal{D}_{X}[s]
-linear operator and since
t\cdot P^{(m)}_{i}\subseteq W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}\subseteq P^{(m)}_{i}
, it follows that each

P_{i}
is a
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}
.
We put
V^{m}\mathcal{M}=W^{m}\mathcal{M}
and for

1\leq i\leq d
, we define the
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
V^{m+\alpha_{i}}\mathcal{M}
such that
W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}\subseteq V^{m+\alpha_{i}}\mathcal{M}
and

V^{m+\alpha_{i}}\mathcal{M}/W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}=P_{i}\oplus\ldots\oplus P_{d}
. Note that by definition we have

W^{m}\mathcal{M}=V^{m}\mathcal{M}=V^{m+\alpha_{1}}\mathcal{M}\supseteq V^{m+\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{M}\supseteq\ldots\supseteq V^{m+\alpha_{d}}\mathcal{M}\supseteq W^{m+1}\mathcal{M}=V^{m+1}\mathcal{M}.

We extend this filtration to all rational numbers such that
V^{\lambda}\mathcal{M}
takes constant value for
\lambda
in each interval
(m,m+\alpha_{1}],(m+\alpha_{1},m+\alpha_{2}],\ldots,(m+\alpha_{d},m+1]
.
Checking that this is indeed a
V
-filtration corresponding to
t
is a straightforward exercise.
∎

Remark 5.14

(The case when
p_{W}
has roots in
(0,1]
)
.

If
W^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
is a pre-
V
-filtration on
\mathcal{M}
with respect to
t
such that the polynomial
p_{W}
has all roots in
(0,1]
, then a similar argument to the one above shows that
\mathcal{M}
has a
V
-filtration
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
with respect to
t
and

W^{i}\mathcal{M}=V^{>i}\mathcal{M}\quad\text{ for all }\quad i\in{\mathbf{Z}}.

We now turn to the proof of the result relating
b
-functions and
V
-filtrations.

Proof of Theorem
5.11
.

In order to prove the assertion in i), note first that
there is
\beta
such that

(34)

V^{\beta}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})\cap\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w\subseteq V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w.

Indeed, by Proposition
3.19
, the
V
-filtration with respect to
t
on

\mathcal{M}_{w}:=\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w
is the restriction of the
V
-filtration on
\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
.
Since
V^{1}\mathcal{M}_{w}
is finitely generated over
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
, it follows that there is
\gamma
such that

V^{1}\mathcal{M}_{w}\subseteq V^{\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w
. We conclude that if
\beta\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{>1}
is such that

\beta+\gamma\geq 2
, then

V^{\beta}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})\cap\mathcal{M}_{w}=V^{\beta}\mathcal{M}_{w}=t^{\beta-1}\cdot V^{1}\mathcal{M}_{w}\subseteq(t^{\beta-1}\cdot V^{\gamma}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}})\cdot w\subseteq V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w,

hence (
34
) holds.

Since
(s+\gamma)
is nilpotent on
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\gamma}(\iota_{+}\mathcal{M})

for all
\gamma\in{\mathbf{Q}}
and since the
V
-filtration is discrete, it follows that if
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}
are the rational numbers
\gamma
with

\alpha\leq\gamma<\beta
and with
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\gamma}(\iota_{+}\mathcal{M})\neq 0
, then there is
N\geq 1
such that

(s+\gamma_{1})^{N}\cdots(s+\gamma_{r})^{N}w\in V^{\beta}\iota_{+}\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w\subseteq V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w.

This implies that
b_{w}(s)
divides
\prod_{i=1}^{r}(s+\gamma_{i})^{N}
, proving the assertion in i).

In order to prove the statement in ii), we see that by Proposition
5.13
, it is enough to show that
\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
has a pre-
V
-filtration
with respect to
t
. For every
j\in{\mathbf{Z}}
, let

W^{j}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M}):=\sum_{\ell=1}^{r}V^{j}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot(w_{\ell}\otimes\delta)\subseteq\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M}).

Since
w_{1},\ldots,w_{r}
generate
\mathcal{M}
over
\mathcal{D}_{X}
, it follows that
w_{1}\otimes\delta,\ldots,w_{r}\otimes\delta
generate
\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})

over
\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
, hence
W^{\bullet}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
is exhaustive.
It is also clear that it satisfies conditions a), b), and c) in Definition
5.12
.
In order to check condition d), let
b_{\ell}
be the
b
-function of
w_{\ell}\otimes\delta
, so
we have
b_{\ell}(-\partial_{t}t)\cdot V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}(w_{\ell}\otimes\delta)\subseteq V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}(w_{\ell}\otimes\delta)
for all
\ell

(see Remark
5.7
). This implies that if
p(x)=\prod_{\ell=1}^{r}b_{\ell}(-x)
, then

p(\partial_{t}t)\cdot W^{0}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})\subseteq W^{1}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
.
Moreover, it follows from the definition of
W^{\bullet}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
and the formulas (
24
) and (
25
) that for all
m\geq 0
, we have

W^{m}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})=t^{m}\cdot W^{0}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
and
W^{-m}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\partial_{t}^{j}\cdot W^{0}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
.
Using Lemma
3.2
, it is then straightforward to deduce that

p(\partial_{t}t+m)\cdot W^{-m}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})\subseteq W^{-m+1}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
for all
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}
, hence
W^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}

is a pre-
V
-filtration on
\mathcal{M}
. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

In fact, once we know that a
\mathcal{D}
-module has a
V
-filtration, this can be characterized via
b
-functions by the condition in Theorem
5.11
i).
This was first shown by Sabbah
[
Sabbah
]
.

Corollary 5.15
.

If a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
, then for every
\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}}
,
V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
consists of those sections
w\in\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})

with the property that all roots of
b_{w}
are
\leq-\alpha
.

Proof.

We have already seen in Theorem
5.11
i) that if
w\in V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
, then all roots of
b_{w}
are rational numbers
\leq-\alpha
. Conversely,
suppose that all roots of
b_{w}
are
\leq-\alpha
. Let
\beta\in{\mathbf{Q}}
be such that
w\in V^{\beta}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
. If
\beta\geq\alpha
, then we are done. If
\beta<\alpha
, since the

V
-filtration is discrete, we may assume that
w\not\in V^{>\beta}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
and aim for a contradiction. Since
b_{w}(s)w\in V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot w\subseteq V^{\beta+1}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
, we have
b_{w}(s)\overline{w}=0
in
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\beta}\big(\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})\big)
. Since
s+\beta
is nilpotent on
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\beta}(\iota_{+}\mathcal{M})
,
while
b_{w}(s)\overline{w}=0
and
b_{w}(s)=\prod_{i=1}^{r}(s+\alpha_{i})
with
\alpha_{i}\geq\alpha>\beta
for all
i
, we conclude that

\overline{w}=0
in
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\beta}(\iota_{+}\mathcal{M})
, a contradiction.
∎

Remark 5.16

(The roots of the
b
-functions vs. the jumps in the
V
-filtration)
.

With the notation in Theorem
5.11
ii), if

R=\bigcup_{i=1}^{r}\big\{\lambda\in{\mathbf{Q}}\mid b_{w_{i}}(-\lambda)=0\big\},

then

R\subseteq\big\{\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}}\mid{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}(\iota_{+}\mathcal{M})\neq 0\big\}\subseteq R+{\mathbf{Z}}.

Indeed, the first inclusion follows from assertion i) in the theorem, while the second inclusion follows from the proof of assertion ii) in the theorem.

Example 5.17

(
b
-functions for invertible
f
)
.

If
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
is invertible and
\iota\colon X\hookrightarrow X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}
is the corresponding graph embedding, then it follows from
Remark
4.5
that for every coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
, we have a
V
-filtration given by

V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})=\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
for all
\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}}
. In this case it follows from Corollary
5.15
that
b_{w}=1
for every

w\in\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
.

Example 5.18

(Holonomic
\mathcal{D}
-module without a
V
-filtration)
.

Let
X={\mathbf{A}}^{1}={\rm Spec}\big(k[x]\big)
and
f=x
.
The
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{D}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}/\mathcal{D}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}\cdot(\partial_{x}x-\lambda),\quad\text{where}\quad\lambda\in k\smallsetminus{\mathbf{Q}},

is holonomic and

f
acts bijectively on
\mathcal{M}
. It is easy to see that the
b
-function of
\overline{1}\otimes\delta
is
b(s)=(s+\lambda)
,
hence
\mathcal{M}
does not have a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
by Theorem
5.11
.

Example 5.19

(Existence of
V
-filtration for
\mathcal{O}_{X}
)
.

Since the Bernstein-Sato polynomial
b_{f}(s)
has rational roots by
[
Kashiwara
]
, it follows from Theorem
5.11

that
\mathcal{O}_{X}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
. Since the roots of
b_{f}(s)
are negative, we deduce using
Corollary
5.15
that
\delta\in V^{>0}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
.

A consequence of Theorem
5.11
is that the subcategory of
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules that have a
V
-filtration with respect to
f

is closed under extensions:

Corollary 5.20
.

Given a short exact sequence of coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules

0\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\to 0,

if
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
have a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
, then so does
\mathcal{M}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
3.16
, we may and will assume that
X
is affine.
After applying
\iota_{+}
, we get an exact sequence

0\to\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\hookrightarrow\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime})\to 0.

We use the criterion for the existence of
V
-filtrations with respect to
f
in Theorem
5.11
.
Therefore it is enough to show that every element
u\in\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
has a
b
-function with roots in
{\mathbf{Q}}
.
Since
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
, we have a polynomial
b_{1}(s)
with roots in
{\mathbf{Q}}
and
Q_{1}\in V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
such that

b_{1}(s)u-Q_{1}u\in\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
. Since
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
, it follows that we have a polynomial
b_{2}(s)
with roots in
{\mathbf{Q}}
and
Q_{2}\in V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
such that

b_{2}(s)\big(b_{1}(s)u-Q_{1}u\big)=Q_{2}\big(b_{1}(s)u-Q_{1}u\big).

Therefore
b_{1}(s)b_{2}(s)u\in V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}u
, hence
u
has a
b
-function that divides
b_{1}b_{2}
, and thus has rational roots.
∎

Corollary 5.21
.

If
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
is the inclusion of the complement of the hypersurface defined by
f
and
if
\mathcal{M}
is a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module such that
\mathcal{M}|_{U}
is holonomic, then
\mathcal{M}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
if and only if
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:=j_{+}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
. Moreover, in this case we have
V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})=V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
for all
\alpha>0
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{M}|_{U}
is holonomic, we know that
j_{+}(\mathcal{M}|_{U})
is holonomic, hence coherent. The assertion in the statement then follows from
Corollaries
3.20
and

5.20
, using the fact that the kernel and the cokernel of the canonical morphism
\gamma\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
are supported on the hypersurface defined by
f
, and thus have a
V
-filtration with
respect to
f
by Example
4.6
. Moreover, these satisfy

V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}\big({\rm ker}(\gamma)\big)=0=V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}\big({\rm coker}(\gamma)\big)\quad\text{for all}\quad\alpha>0,

which gives the last assertion in the corollary.
∎

Example 5.22
.

For every nonzero
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
, the
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{O}_{X}[1/f]
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
f
and

V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}(\mathcal{O}_{X})=V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}[1/f]\big)\quad\text{for all}\quad\alpha>0.

This follows from the above corollary and Example
5.19
.

We end this section by stating without proof a result giving estimates for the roots of certain
b
-functions in terms of a log resolution of
(X,H)
,
where
H
is the hypersurface defined by
f
. Recall that a log resolution of
(X,H)
is a projective morphism

\pi\colon Y\to X
that is an isomorphism over
X\smallsetminus H
, with
Y
smooth, and such that
\pi^{*}(H)
is a simple normal crossing divisor. We write

(35)

\pi^{*}(H)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}a_{i}E_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad K_{Y/X}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}k_{i}E_{i},

where
K_{Y/X}
is the effective divisor on
Y
defined by the determinant of
\pi^{*}(\Omega_{X})\to\Omega_{Y}
.
Such a log resolution exists by Hironaka’s theorem.

Theorem 5.23
.

Let
X
be a smooth, irreducible algebraic variety and
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
nonzero, defining the hypersurface
H
.
We also consider a nonnegative integer
m
and a nonzero
g\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
.
Given a log resolution
\pi\colon Y\to X
of
(X,H)
, with the notation in (
35
), the
following hold:

i)

Every root of
b_{f}
is of the form
-\tfrac{k_{i}+\ell}{a_{i}}
, for some
i
with
1\leq i\leq N
and some positive integer
\ell
.

ii)

Every root of
b_{g\partial_{t}^{m}\delta}
is
\leq-\min\big\{1,\tfrac{k_{i}+b_{i}+1}{a_{i}}-m\mid 1\leq i\leq N\big\}
, where
b_{i}
the coefficient of
E_{i}
in
\pi^{*}\big({\rm div}(g)\big)
.

iii)

Every root of
b_{g\delta}
is
\leq-\min\big\{\tfrac{k_{i}+b_{i}+1}{a_{i}}\mid 1\leq i\leq N\big\}
.

iv)

Every root of
b_{\partial_{t}^{m}\delta}
is either a negative integer or it is of the form
m-\tfrac{k_{i}+\ell}{a_{i}}
, for some
i
with
1\leq i\leq N
and some positive integer
\ell
.
Furthermore, if
H
is reduced and the strict transforms of the components of
H
on
X
are disjoint
5
5

5

This condition can always be achieved after performing finitely many further
blow-ups.
, then we may take
i
such that the divisor
E_{i}
is exceptional.

The assertion in i) was proved by Lichtin
[
Lichtin
]
, improving on Kashiwara’s method for proving the rationality of the roots of
b_{f}
in
[
Kashiwara
]
. The assertions in
ii)-iv) were proved in
[
DM
]
, following Lichtin’s argument. We will discuss some applications to invariants of singularities in Section
10
.

6.
An example: weighted homogeneous, isolated singularities

In this section we discuss the
V
-filtration and the Bernstein-Sato polynomial for weighted homogeneous polynomials, with isolated singularities.
We consider
X={\mathbf{A}}^{n}
, with
\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)=R=k[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
and we write
D_{R}
for the ring of differential operators of
R
.
A polynomial
f\in R
is
weighted homogeneous
if the following condition holds:
there are
w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}\in{\mathbf{Q}}_{>0}
such that if for a monomial
x^{u}=x_{1}^{u_{1}}\cdots x_{n}^{u_{n}}\in R
we put
\rho(x^{u})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{i}w_{i}
, then
there is
d
such that
f=\sum_{\rho(x^{u})=d}c_{u}x^{u}
(once the
w_{i}
are fixed, we will refer to such polynomials as being

w
-homogeneous of degree
d
and write
\rho(f)=d
). After possibly rescaling all the
w_{i}
by the same positive rational number, we may and will assume that

\rho(f)=1
. We put
|w|:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}
.
We consider on
R
the filtration given by shifting by
|w|
the filtration induced by
\rho
: for every
q\in{\mathbf{Q}}
, we put

R_{\geq q}=\bigoplus_{\rho(u)+|w|\geq q}kx^{u}\quad\text{and}\quad R_{>q}=\bigoplus_{\rho(u)+|w|>q}kx^{u}.

Note that these are ideals of
R
.

A special role in our setting is played by the operator
\theta=\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}x_{i}\partial_{i}
and by
\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\partial_{i}x_{i}=\theta+|w|
.
Note that if
h
is
w
-homogeneous, then
\theta(h)=\rho(h)h
, hence an easy computation gives

(36)

\theta\cdot hf^{s}=\big(s+\rho(h)\big)hf^{s}\quad\text{and}\quad(w_{1}\partial_{1}x_{1}+\ldots+w_{n}\partial_{n}x_{n})\cdot hf^{s}=\big(s+\rho(h)+|w|\big)hf^{s}.

We also note that since
f=\theta(f)
, it follows that
f\in J_{f}:=\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{1}},\ldots,\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{n}}\big)
, hence the zero-locus of
V(J_{f})
is precisely the singular locus of the hypersurface
Z
defined by
f
.
Furthermore,
Z
has an isolated singularity at
0
if and only if
V(J_{f})\subseteq\{0\}
. Indeed, if
m
is a positive integer such that

mw_{i}\in{\mathbf{Z}}
for all
i
, then the
k^{*}
-action on
X
given by
\lambda\cdot(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})=(\lambda^{mw_{1}}u_{1},\ldots,\lambda^{mw_{n}}u_{n})
preserves

Z
; if
P
is a singular point of
Z
different from the origin, then
k^{*}\cdot P
is a 1-dimensional subset of the singular locus of
Z

whose closure contains
0
.

6.1.
The
V
-filtration for weighted homogeneous polynomials with isolated singularities

From now on we assume that
f
is
w
-homogeneous, of degree
1
, with an isolated singularity at
0
.
The following result describes the
V
-filtration on
\iota_{+}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
in this case. We denote by
\lceil\alpha\rceil
the smallest integer
\geq\alpha
.

Theorem 6.1
.

If we define
W^{\alpha}\subseteq\iota_{+}(R)
by

(37)

W^{\alpha}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\sum_{j\geq 0}D_{R}\cdot R_{\geq\alpha+j}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta,&\text{if}\,\,\alpha\leq 1;\\[5.69054pt]
t^{\lceil\alpha\rceil-1}W^{\alpha-\lceil\alpha\rceil+1}\iota_{+}(R),&\text{if}\,\,\alpha>1,\end{array}\right.

then
V^{\alpha}\iota_{+}(R)=W^{\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}}
.

We note that a more precise statement, involving also the Hodge filtration,
is given for the microlocal
V
-filtration in
[
Saito_filtration
, (4.2.1)]
. We give an elementary proof of the above result, by showing that the filtration in the theorem satisfies
the definition of the
V
-filtration.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

We need to show that
(W^{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}}}

satisfies the definition of the
V
-filtration. Since
R_{\geq\beta}\subseteq R_{\geq\gamma}
for
\beta\geq\gamma
, we see
that in order to check that
W^{\bullet}
is a decreasing filtration,
it is enough to show that for every
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{>0}
and
0<\epsilon\ll 1
, we have

W^{m+\epsilon}=t^{m}\cdot W^{\epsilon}\subseteq W^{m}=t^{m-1}W^{1}.

This follows using the fact that
f
is
w
-homogeneous with
\rho(f)=1
:

t\cdot R_{\geq\epsilon+j}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta\subseteq f\cdot R_{\geq\epsilon+j}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta+R_{\geq\epsilon+j}\partial_{t}^{j-1}\delta\subseteq R_{1+j}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta+R_{\geq j}\partial_{t}^{j-1}\delta\subseteq W^{1}.

The fact that
W^{\bullet}
is exhaustive
follows from the fact that
R_{\geq\alpha+j}=R
if
\alpha\leq-j
. Note also that if
\ell
is such that
\ell w_{i}\in{\mathbf{Z}}
for all
i
,
then
R_{\geq\lambda}
is constant for
\lambda
in any interval of the form
\big(\tfrac{i}{\ell},\tfrac{i+1}{\ell}\big]
, with
i\in{\mathbf{Z}}
;
therefore the same holds for
W^{\lambda}
.

Let us show that

(38)

t\cdot W^{\alpha}\subseteq W^{\alpha+1}\quad\text{for all}\quad\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}},

with equality for
\alpha>0
.
Of course, the assertion for
\alpha>0
holds by definition, hence we may assume
\alpha\leq 0
.
By definition of
W^{\alpha}
, it is enough to show that for every
j\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, we have

t\cdot R_{\geq\alpha+j}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta\subseteq W^{\alpha+1}
. This follows using the fact that

f
-is
w
-homogeneous, with
\rho(f)=1
:

t\cdot R_{\geq\alpha+j}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta\subseteq f\cdot R_{\geq\alpha+j}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta+R_{\geq\alpha+j}\partial_{t}^{j-1}\delta\subseteq R_{\geq\alpha+j+1}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta+R_{\geq\alpha+j}\partial_{t}^{j-1}\delta\subseteq W^{\alpha+1}.

The inclusion

(39)

\partial_{t}\cdot W^{\alpha}\subseteq W^{\alpha-1}\quad\text{for}\quad\alpha\leq 1

follows directly from the definition.
Suppose now that
\alpha\in(m,m+1]
for some integer
m\geq 2
, in which case we have

\partial_{t}\cdot W^{\alpha}=\partial_{t}\cdot t^{m}W^{\alpha-m}\subseteq t^{m}\partial_{t}\cdot W^{\alpha-m}+t^{m-1}W^{\alpha-m}\subseteq t^{m}\cdot W^{\alpha-m-1}+t^{m-1}\cdot W^{\alpha-m}\subseteq W^{\alpha-1},

where we use (
38
) and (
39
).

We next show that
\partial_{t}t-\alpha
is nilpotent on
{\rm Gr}_{W}^{\alpha}=W^{\alpha}/W^{>\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}}
.
Suppose first that
\alpha<1
. It is enough to show that if
h\in R
is
w
-homogeneous, with
\rho(h)+|w|=\alpha+j
,
then
(\partial_{t}t-\alpha)h\partial_{t}^{j}\delta\in D_{R}\cdot R_{>\alpha+j}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta
. To see this, note that by Lemma
3.2
and
(
36
), we have

(\partial_{t}t-\alpha)h\partial_{t}^{j}\delta=\partial_{t}^{j}(\partial_{t}t-\alpha-j)h\delta=-\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\partial_{x_{i}}x_{i}h\partial_{t}^{j}\delta\in D_{R}\cdot R_{>\alpha+j}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta,

where we use the fact that
x_{i}h\in R_{>\alpha+j}
for all
i
, due to the fact that
w_{i}>0
.

We deduce that
(\partial_{t}t-1)^{2}\cdot{\rm Gr}_{W}^{1}=0
. Indeed, we have already seen that
\partial_{t}W^{1}\subseteq W^{0}
, so by what we have already proved,
we have
(\partial_{t}t)\partial_{t}\cdot W^{1}\subseteq W^{>0}
. We thus get

(\partial_{t}t-1)^{2}\cdot W^{1}=t(\partial_{t}t\partial_{t})\cdot W^{1}\subseteq t\cdot W^{>0}\subseteq W^{>1}

by (
38
). Note now that if
\alpha\in(m,m+1]
, for a positive integer
m
, we know that

(\partial_{t}t-\alpha+m)^{k}\cdot W^{\alpha-m}\subseteq W^{>\alpha-m},\quad\text{with}\quad k\in\{1,2\},

and
using the definition of
W^{\alpha}
and Lemma
3.2
, we obtain

(\partial_{t}t-\alpha)^{k}\cdot W^{\alpha}=(\partial_{t}t-\alpha)^{k}t^{m}\cdot W^{\alpha-m}=t^{m}(\partial_{t}t-\alpha+m)^{k}\cdot W^{\alpha-m}\subseteq t^{m}\cdot W^{>\alpha-m}=W^{>\alpha}.

In order to complete the proof of the theorem, it is enough to show that each
W^{\alpha}
is a finitely generated
D_{R}[t\partial_{t}]
-module;
in fact, we will show that it is a finitely generated
D_{R}
-module.
Since
W^{\alpha}\subseteq W^{1}
for
\alpha\geq 1
and
W^{\alpha}=t^{\lceil\alpha\rceil-1}W^{\alpha-\lceil\alpha\rceil+1}
for
\alpha>1
, it follows that
it is enough to show that
W^{1}
is a finitely generated
D_{R}
-module. Since
\dim_{k}(R/J_{f})<\infty
and
w_{i}>0
for all
i
, it follows that
there is
N
such that
R_{\geq N+1}\subseteq J_{f}
. We claim that in this case

R_{\geq j+1}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta\subseteq D_{R}\cdot R_{\geq j}\partial_{t}^{j-1}\delta
for all
j\geq N
.
Indeed, given
g\in R_{\geq j+1}
, which we may assume
w
-homogeneous, we have
h\in J_{f}
, hence we can write
h=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}g_{i}
,
with
g_{i}\in R_{\geq j+w_{i}}
(recall that
\rho(f)=1
, hence
\rho\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\big)=1-w_{i}
). Since we can write

h\partial_{t}^{j}\delta=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{x_{i}}\cdot g_{i}\partial_{t}^{j-1}\delta-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tfrac{\partial g}{\partial x_{i}}\partial_{t}^{j-1}\delta

and
g_{i},\tfrac{\partial g_{i}}{\partial x_{i}}\in R_{\geq j}
, this proves our assertion. This in turn implies that

W^{1}=\sum_{j=0}^{N}D_{R}\cdot R_{\geq j+1}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta,

hence
W^{1}
is a finitely generated
D_{R}
-module. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

6.2.
The
b
-function of weighted homogeneous polynomials with isolated singularities

We next turn to the description of the Bernstein-Sato polynomial of
f
, that will require a bit more work.
We keep the assumption that
f
is
w
-homogeneous, of degree
1
, with an isolated singularity at
0
.
Recall that
R/J_{f}
is a finite-dimensional
k
-vector space.
We put

\Sigma(f)=\{\rho(g)\mid g\in R\smallsetminus J_{f},\,g\,\,\text{is}\,\,w-\text{homogeneous}\}.

Note that this is a finite set: if
x^{u_{1}},\ldots,x^{u_{r}}
are monomials whose classes in
R/J_{f}
form a basis,
then

\Sigma(f)=\big\{\rho(u_{1}),\ldots,\rho(u_{r})\big\}
. Indeed, the only thing to note is that if
h\not\in J_{f}

is
w
-homogeneous and if we write
h=\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}x^{u_{i}}+g
, where
c_{i}\in k
for all
i
and
g\in J_{f}
, since
J_{f}
is generated
by
w
-homogeneous elements, we may assume that
g
is
w
-homogeneous, with
\rho(h)=\rho(g)=\rho(x^{u_{i}})

for all
i
such that
c_{i}\neq 0
(note also that some
c_{i}
must be nonzero since
g\not\in J_{f}
).

Theorem 6.2

(
[
BGM
]
)
.

The Bernstein-Sato polynomial of
f
is given by

(40)

b_{f}(s)=(s+1)\cdot\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f)}\big(s+\lambda+|w|).

Before giving the proof of the theorem, we need to make some preparations.
We begin with the following

Lemma 6.3
.

If
h\in R
is
w
-homogeneous, then

(41)

\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f),\lambda\geq\rho(h)}\big(s+\lambda+|w|)hf^{s}\in D_{R}\cdot J_{f}f^{s}.

Proof.

Note first that (
41
) clearly holds if
h\in J_{f}
, hence from now on we assume that
h\not\in J_{f}
and argue
by descending induction on
\rho(h)
. Suppose first that
h
is such that
\rho(h)
is the largest element of
\Sigma(f)
, in which case we have
x_{i}h\in J_{f}
for
1\leq i\leq n
.
In this case it follows from (
36
) that we have

\big(s+\rho(h)+|w|\big)hf^{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\partial_{i}(x_{i}hf^{s})\in D_{R}\cdot J_{f}f^{s}.

Suppose now that
h\not\in J_{f}
and we know that (
41
) holds for all
h^{\prime}
that are
w
-homogeneous, with
\rho(h^{\prime})>\rho(h)
. In particular, it holds
for
x_{i}h
for all
i
, hence

\prod_{\lambda^{\prime}\in\Sigma(f),\lambda^{\prime}>\rho(h)}\big(s+\lambda^{\prime}+|w|\big)x_{i}hf^{s}\in D_{R}\cdot J_{f}f^{s}\quad\text{for}\quad 1\leq i\leq n.

We thus deduce using (
36
) that

\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f),\lambda\geq\rho(h)}\big(s+\lambda+|w|\big)hf^{s}=\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f),\lambda>\rho(h)}\big(s+\lambda+|w|\big)\cdot\big(s+\rho(h)+|w|\big)hf^{s}

=\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f),\lambda>\rho(h)}\big(s+\lambda+|w|\big)\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\partial_{i}(x_{i}h)f^{s}\subseteq D_{R}\cdot J_{f}f^{s}.

This completes the proof of the induction step.
∎

Following
[
Yano
, Theorem 2.19]
, we next prove a general result concerning
{\rm Ann}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(f^{s})
for isolated hypersurface singularities.
As suggested by the functional equation (
31
), an understanding of this annihilator could be useful for computing
b_{f}(s)
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth, irreducible variety and
P\in X
a point lying on the hypersurface
Z
defined by
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
.
If
Z
has an isolated singularity at
P
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
are algebraic coordinates in a neighborhood of
P
, then
the left ideal

{\rm Ann}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(f^{s})=\{Q\in\mathcal{D}_{X}\mid Q\cdot f^{s}=0\}

is generated in a neighborhood of
P
by
\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}-\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{j}}\partial_{x_{i}}
, for
1\leq i<j\leq n
.
In particular, we have

\{Q\in\mathcal{D}_{X}\mid Q\cdot f^{s}\in\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot J_{f}f^{s}\}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot J_{f}\quad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{D}_{X},

where
J_{f}=\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{1}},\ldots,\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{n}}\big)
.

Proof.

It is clear that the left ideal
I
of
\mathcal{D}_{X}
generated by
\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}-\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{j}}\partial_{x_{i}}
, for
1\leq i<j\leq n
,
is contained in
{\rm Ann}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(f^{s})
, hence we need to prove the reverse inclusion. The assertion is easy to check when
P
is a smooth point of
Z
.
Indeed, if
\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}(P)\neq 0
and
Q\in{\rm Ann}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(f^{s})
, after writing
Q
modulo the left ideal generated by

\partial_{x_{j}}-\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\big)^{-1}\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{j}}\partial{x_{i}}
, we may assume that
Q\in\mathcal{O}_{X}[\partial_{x_{i}}]
and we need to show that
Q=0
.
This follows from the fact that
\partial_{x_{i}}^{m}f^{s}=Q_{m}f^{s}
, where
Q_{m}\in\mathcal{O}_{X}[1/f,s]
is a polynomial of degree
m
in
s
.

From now on we assume that
P
is a singular point of
Z
. After possibly replacing
X
by a suitable open neighborhood of
P
, we may assume that
X
is affine, with

\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)=R
,
that
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
are defined on
X
,
and that
\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{1}},\ldots,\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{n}}
form a regular sequence in
R
. We need to show that if
Q\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{D}_{X})
is such that
Q\cdot f^{s}=0
, then

Q
lies in
I
. We argue by induction on the order
q
of
Q
, the case
q=0
being trivial: in this case it is clear that
Q=0
. Note that for every
\alpha\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{n}
,
we can write
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{s}=g_{\alpha}f^{s-|\alpha|}
, where
g_{\alpha}\in R[s]
has degree
|\alpha|
in
s
, with the coefficient of the top degree term equal to

\prod_{i}\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\big)^{\alpha_{i}}
.
We can write
Q=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq q}Q_{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}
, with
Q_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{O}_{X}
for all
\alpha
, and we see that

\sum_{|\alpha|=q}Q_{\alpha}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\big)^{\alpha_{i}}=0.

Since
\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{1}},\ldots,\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{n}}
is a regular sequence in
R
, it follows that in the ring
R[y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}]
we can write

\sum_{|\alpha|=q}Q_{\alpha}y_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\cdots y_{n}^{\alpha_{n}}=\sum_{i<j}h_{i,j}\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}y_{j}-\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{j}}y_{i}\big),

for some
h_{i,j}\in R[y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}]
that are homogeneous in
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}
, of degree
q-1
.
If
h_{i,j}=\sum_{\beta}h_{i,j,\beta}y^{\beta}
, with
h_{i,j,\beta}\in R
, then the difference

\widetilde{Q}=Q-\sum_{i,j,\beta}h_{i,j,\beta}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}-\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{j}}\partial_{x_{i}}\big)\in D_{R}

has order
q-1
and
\widetilde{Q}f^{s}=0
. We conclude by induction that
\widetilde{Q}\in I
and thus
Q\in I
, too.

The last assertion in the proposition is clear once we note that

\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}-\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{j}}\partial_{x_{i}}=\partial_{x_{j}}\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}-\tfrac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{i}}-\partial_{x_{i}}\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{j}}+\tfrac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}=\partial_{x_{j}}\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}-\partial_{x_{i}}\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{j}}\in\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot J_{f}.

∎

We can now give the proof of the formula for the Bernstein-Sato polynomial for weighted homogeneous polynomials with an isolated singularity, following

[
BGM
]
.

Proof of Theorem
6.2
.

We first show that
b_{f}(s)
divides
(s+1)\cdot\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f)}\big(s+\lambda+|w|)
, or equivalently, that

(42)

(s+1)\cdot\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f)}\big(s+\lambda+|w|)f^{s}\in D_{R}[s]f^{s+1}.

This follows by applying Lemma
6.3
for
h=1
: we can write

\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f),\lambda\geq 0}\big(s+\lambda+|w|)f^{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{i}\cdot\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}f^{s}

for some
P_{1},\ldots,P_{n}\in D_{R}
, in which case, we have

(s+1)\cdot\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f)}\big(s+\lambda+|w|)f^{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(s+1)P_{i}\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}f^{s}=(P_{1}\partial_{1}+\ldots+P_{n}\partial_{n})\cdot f^{s+1},

hence (
42
) holds.

We next need to show that
(s+1)\cdot\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma(f)}\big(s+\lambda+|w|)
divides
b_{f}(s)
, or equivalently, that for every
h\in R\smallsetminus J_{f}
that is
w
-homogeneous,
if
\lambda=\rho(h)
, then
\widetilde{b}_{f}\big(-\lambda-|w|\big)=0
.
By definition of the reduced Bernstein-Sato polynomial, we can write

(43)

(s+1)\widetilde{b}_{f}(s)f^{s}=P\cdot f^{s+1},

for some
P\in D_{R}[s]
.
Let us write
P=\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{i}\partial_{i}+Q
, where

Q\in R[s]
and
P_{i}\in D_{R}[s]
. The equality (
43
) becomes

(s+1)\widetilde{b}_{f}(s)f^{s}=Q\cdot f^{s+1}+(s+1)\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{i}\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\cdot f^{s}.

We can thus write
Q=(s+1)T
for some
T\in R[s]
and we have

\widetilde{b}_{f}(s)f^{s}=Q_{0}\cdot f^{s+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{i}\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\cdot f^{s}\in D_{R}[s]\cdot J_{f}f^{s},

where we use the fact that
f\in J_{f}
.

Note also that

(44)

D_{R}[s]\cdot J_{f}f^{s}\subseteq D_{R}\cdot J_{f}f^{s}.

Indeed, we have
sf^{s}=\theta f^{s}
and

\big[\theta,\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\big]=\theta\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\big)=(1-w_{i})\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}

(due to the fact that
\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}
is
w
-homogeneous with
\rho\big(\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\big)=1-w_{i}
),
hence

s\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}f^{s}=(\theta-1+w_{i})\tfrac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}f^{s}.

We thus conclude that

\widetilde{b}_{f}(s)f^{s}\in D_{R}\cdot J_{f}f^{s}
, hence also

(45)

\widetilde{b}_{f}(s)hf^{s}\in D_{R}\cdot J_{f}f^{s}.

Note now that by (
36
), we have

\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\partial_{i}x_{i}\right)\cdot hf^{s}=\big(s+\lambda+|w|\big)hf^{s}.

Let us write
\widetilde{b}_{f}(s)=\big(s+\lambda+|w|\big)q(s)+a
, for some
q\in k[s]
and some
a\in k
.
Our goal is to show that
a=0
. Since

\widetilde{b}_{f}(s)hf^{s}=ahf^{s}+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\partial_{i}x_{i}\right)\cdot q(s)hf^{s}\in D_{R}\cdot J_{f}f^{s}

and since

q(s)f^{s}\in D_{R}\cdot f^{s}
(this follows using again
sf^{s}=\theta f^{s}
), we conclude that there is

P\in D_{R}
such that

ahf^{s}+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\partial_{i}x_{i}\right)\cdot Pf^{s}\in D_{R}\cdot J_{f}f^{s},

in which case, we conclude from Proposition
6.4
that

ah+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\partial_{i}x_{i}\right)\cdot P\in D_{R}\cdot J_{f}.

Since we have a direct sum decomposition
D_{R}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{n}}\partial^{\alpha}R

that induces the decomposition
D_{R}\cdot J_{f}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{n}}\partial^{\alpha}J_{f}
,
we conclude that
ah\in J_{f}
. Since
h\not\in J_{f}
, it follows that
a=0
, hence
\widetilde{b}_{f}\big(-\lambda-|w|\big)=0
.
This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

7.
Duality and
V
-filtrations

Our goal in this section is to discuss the compatibility of
V
-filtrations with duality.
Let
X
be a smooth, irreducible,
(n+1)
-dimensional algebraic variety. Recall that on
X
we have
the duality functor
(-)^{*}
, see (
6
), such that
for a left
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
,
the right
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module corresponding to
\mathcal{H}^{j}(\mathcal{M}^{*})
is
{\mathcal{E}xt}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}^{j+n+1}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{D}_{X})
.
The following theorem allows us to relate the graded pieces of the
V
-filtration to the duality functor.

Theorem 7.1
.

(
[
MaisonobeMebkhout
, Prop. 4.6-2]
,
[
SaitoDuality
, Theorem 1.6]
)
Let
X
be a smooth, irreducible algebraic variety and
H\subseteq X
a smooth hypersurface, defined by
t\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
. If
\mathcal{M}
is a
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module that has a
V
-filtration
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
along
H
, then
all
\mathcal{H}^{j}(\mathcal{M}^{*})
have such a
V
-filtration and
we have the following functorial isomorphisms for all
j\in\mathbf{Z}
:

i)

\delta_{1}\colon{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}\big(\mathcal{H}^{j}(\mathcal{M}^{*})\big)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{H}^{j}\big(({\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}\mathcal{M})^{*}\big)
,

ii)

\delta_{0}\colon{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}\big(\mathcal{H}^{j}(\mathcal{M}^{*})\big)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{H}^{j}\big(({\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}\mathcal{M})^{*}\big)
,

iii)

\delta_{\alpha}\colon{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\lambda}\big((\mathcal{H}^{j}(\mathcal{M}^{*})\big)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{H}^{j}\big(({\rm Gr}_{V}^{1-\lambda}\mathcal{M})^{*}\big)
for all
\lambda\in(0,1)

such that if we denote by
N
the nilpotent endomorphism
(s+\lambda)
on
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\lambda}(-)
and
{\rm can},{\rm Var}
are the maps from formulas (
11
)
and (
12
), then

iv)

N^{*}\circ\delta_{\lambda}=\delta_{\lambda}\circ(-N)
,

v)

{\rm can}^{*}\circ\delta_{1}=\delta_{0}\circ{\rm Var}
,

vi)

(-{\rm Var})^{*}\circ\delta_{0}=\delta_{1}\circ{\rm can}
.

We explain the key ingredients in the proof of the above theorem, following the argument in
[
MebkhoutSabbah
,
MaisonobeMebkhout
]
.
Note that a different proof of the same result for Hodge modules, keeping track also of the Hodge filtration, can be found in
[
SaitoDuality
]
.
The main idea is to show that locally, every
\mathcal{D}
-module which admits a
V
-filtration has a resolution by “elementary”
\mathcal{D}
-modules, and then prove the theorem for such modules.

Definition 7.2
.

Let
b(s)\in\mathbf{Q}[s]
be a nonzero polynomial such that
b(-\lambda)=0
implies
\lambda\in[0,1)\cap{\mathbf{Q}}
. We denote by
m
the multiplicity of the root
0
in
b(s)
. Let
Q
be a
p\times q
matrix with entries in
\Gamma(X,V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X})
and
P
a
q\times q
matrix with entries in
\Gamma(X,V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X})
. We denote by
\Lambda=\Lambda(b,P,Q)
the

(p+q)\times(p+q)
-matrix

\begin{pmatrix}s^{m}\text{Id}_{p}&-Q\\
0&(s+1)b(s+1)\text{Id}_{q}-P\end{pmatrix},

and we let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(b,P,Q)
be the cokernel of the
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-linear morphism

\mathcal{D}_{X}^{p}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{X}^{q}\xrightarrow{\cdot\Lambda}\mathcal{D}_{X}^{p}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{X}^{q},

which we call an
elementary
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
.

Lemma 7.3
.

(
[
MaisonobeMebkhout
, Lem. 4.5-2]
)

Suppose that
X
is an affine variety.
If
\mathcal{M}
is a
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module that admits a
V
-filtration along
H
, then
\mathcal{M}
is a quotient of an elementary
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module.

Proof.

Let
b_{0}(s)
be the minimal polynomial of
s=-\partial_{t}t
on
V^{0}\mathcal{M}/V^{1}\mathcal{M}
.
Note that by condition ii) in the definition of the
V
-filtration, all roots of
b_{0}(-s)
lie in
[0,1)\cap{\mathbf{Q}}
.
Let
m
be the multiplicity of the factor
s
in
b_{0}(s)
, let
v_{1},\dots,v_{p}
be global sections of
V^{0}\mathcal{M}
which generate
\ker(s^{m})\subseteq V^{0}\mathcal{M}/V^{1}\mathcal{M}
over
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
,
and let
u_{1},\dots,u_{q}
be global sections of
V^{1}\mathcal{M}
generating it over

V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
. The
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-submodule generated by
u_{1},\dots,u_{q},v_{1},\dots,v_{p}
is
\mathcal{M}
: indeed, it contains
V^{0}\mathcal{M}
since

V^{0}\mathcal{M}\subseteq V^{1}\mathcal{M}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot v_{i}+\partial_{t}\cdot V^{1}\mathcal{M}
, and then we conclude that it is equal to
\mathcal{M}
using
Corollary
3.12
iii).

To understand the relations among these generators, note that by construction we have

(46)

s^{m}v_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}Q_{i,j}u_{j},\quad\text{with}\quad Q_{i,j}\in\Gamma(X,V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}).

Moreover, we can also write

(47)

(s+1)b_{0}(s+1)u_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}P_{i,j}u_{j},\quad\text{where}\quad P_{i,j}\in\Gamma(X,V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X}).

Indeed, since
s^{m}{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}\mathcal{M}=0
, using Lemma
3.2
, we see that

(s+1)^{m+1}\cdot V^{1}\mathcal{M}=t\partial_{t}(s+1)^{m}\cdot V^{1}\mathcal{M}=ts^{m}\partial_{t}\cdot V^{1}\mathcal{M}\subseteq ts^{m}\cdot V^{0}\mathcal{M}\subseteq t\cdot V^{>0}\mathcal{M}\subseteq V^{>1}\mathcal{M}.

Using also the fact that
V^{\alpha+1}\mathcal{M}=t\cdot V^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}
for
\alpha>0
, Lemma
3.2
, and the
definition of
b_{0}(s)
, we conclude that

(s+1)\cdot b_{0}(s+1)\cdot V^{1}\mathcal{M}\subseteq V^{2}\mathcal{M}=t\cdot V^{1}\mathcal{M}\subseteq\sum_{j=1}^{q}V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot u_{j},

giving (
47
). We then deduce from (
46
) and (
47
) that
\mathcal{M}
is a quotient of
\mathcal{E}(b_{0},P,Q)

∎

The following lemma gives more details on the structure of elementary modules.

Lemma 7.4
.

With the notation in Definition
7.2
, if
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(b,P,Q)
, then the following hold:

i)

The
sequence

(48)

0\to\mathcal{D}_{X}^{p}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{X}^{q}\xrightarrow{\cdot\Lambda}\mathcal{D}_{X}^{p}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{X}^{q}\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathcal{E}\to 0

is exact.

ii)

If we put
U^{k}\mathcal{E}:=\pi\big((V^{k}\mathcal{D}_{X})^{p}\oplus(V^{k-1}\mathcal{D}_{X})^{q}\big)
for
k\in{\mathbf{Z}}
, then we have an induced exact sequence

(49)

0\to{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{p}\oplus{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{q}\to{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{p}\oplus{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{q}\to U^{k}\mathcal{E}/U^{k+1}\mathcal{E}\to 0.

iii)

The filtration
U^{\bullet}\mathcal{E}
is a
{\mathbf{Z}}
-indexed
V
-filtration on
\mathcal{E}
with respect to
t
in the sense of Definition
5.12
.
Therefore
\mathcal{E}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
t
, we have
V^{k}\mathcal{E}=U^{k}\mathcal{E}

for every
k\in{\mathbf{Z}}
, and we thus have
an isomorphism of
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}=\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]
-modules

(50)

V^{k}\mathcal{E}/V^{k+1}\mathcal{E}\simeq\left(\frac{\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]}{(s+k)^{m}}\right)^{p}\oplus\left(\frac{\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]}{(s+k)b(s+k)}\right)^{q}.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda=\Lambda(b,P,Q)
be a matrix as in the definition of the elementary module
\mathcal{E}(b,P,Q)
. Assume
(A,B)\in\mathcal{D}_{X}^{p}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{X}^{q}
lies in the kernel of
\cdot\Lambda
, that is, we have

As^{m}=0\quad\text{and}\quad-AQ+B\big((s+1)b(s+1)-P\big)=0.

Since every nonzero element of
k[s]
acts injectively on
\mathcal{D}_{X}
,
it is clear that
A=0
, hence

B(s+1)b(s+1)=BP
. Note also that the nonzero polynomials in
k[s]
act injectively on
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D}_{X})

(this follows easily from the formulas (
7
), (
8
), and (
9
)). Since the entries of
P

lie in
V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X}
, we have
B=0
. Therefore the map
\cdot\Lambda
is injective and (
48
) is exact.

In order to prove ii),
note first that since
Q
has entries in
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
and
P
has entries in
V^{1}\mathcal{D}_{X}
, the map
\cdot\Lambda

induces a map

{\rm Gr}^{k}_{V}(\cdot\Lambda)\colon{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{p}\oplus{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{q}\to{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{p}\oplus{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{q}

represented by the matrix

\begin{pmatrix}s^{m}\text{Id}_{p}&0\\
0&(s+1)b(s+1)\text{Id}_{q}\end{pmatrix}.

We see that this is injective, using again the fact that nonzero polynomials in
k[s]
act injectively on
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D}_{X})
.
The exactness of (
49
) is now clear.

Moreover, we see that

U^{k}\mathcal{E}/U^{k+1}\mathcal{E}\simeq\left(\frac{{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}\mathcal{D}_{X}}{{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}\mathcal{D}_{X}s^{m}}\right)^{p}\oplus\left(\frac{{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k-1}\mathcal{D}_{X}}{{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k-1}\mathcal{D}_{X}(s+1)b(s+1)}\right)^{q}

\simeq\left(\frac{{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}\mathcal{D}_{X}}{(s+k)^{m}{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}\mathcal{D}_{X}}\right)^{p}\oplus\left(\frac{{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k-1}\mathcal{D}_{X}}{(s+k)b(s+k){\rm Gr}_{V}^{k-1}\mathcal{D}_{X}}\right)^{q},

where the second isomorphism follows easily from Lemma
3.2
and the formulas (
7
), (
8
), and (
9
).
This implies that the polynomial
p(x)=xb(x)
, whose roots are all in
[0,1)
, has the property that
p(\partial_{t}t-k)\cdot U^{k}\mathcal{E}\subseteq U^{k+1}\mathcal{E}
for all
k\in{\mathbf{Z}}
. Since it is straightforward to see that

U^{\bullet}\mathcal{E}
also satisfies properties a)-c) in Definition
5.12
, we conclude that
U^{\bullet}\mathcal{E}
is a
{\mathbf{Z}}
-indexed
V
-filtration on
\mathcal{E}

with respect to
t
. The fact that
\mathcal{E}
has a
V
-filtration and
V^{k}\mathcal{E}=U^{k}\mathcal{E}
for all
k\in{\mathbf{Z}}
now follows from Proposition
5.13
.
∎

The free resolution given in Lemma
7.4
allows us to compute the dual of an elementary
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module. As we will see shortly, the dual has its own free resolution and we can use it to compute the
V
-filtration.

Working locally, in what follows we assume that we have a system of coordinates
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},t
on
X
.
Recall that we have the classical adjoint given by (
1
).
For example, we have
{}^{t}s=t\partial_{t}=-s-1
.
For a matrix of differential operators
\Lambda=(\Lambda_{ij})
, we denote by

{}^{t}\Lambda
the matrix
({}^{t}\Lambda_{ji})
,
that is, the transpose of the matrix of classical adjoints of the entires of
\Lambda
.

Lemma 7.5
.

With the notation in Definition
7.2
, if
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(b,P,Q)
is an elementary
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module, with
\Lambda
the matrix of differential operators defining
\mathcal{E}
,
then the following hold:

i)

We have
\mathcal{H}^{i}(\mathcal{E}^{*})=0
for all
i\neq-n
.

ii)

If
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(b,P,Q):=\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathcal{E}^{*})
, then we have an exact sequence

(51)

0\to\mathcal{D}_{X}^{p}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{X}^{q}\xrightarrow{\cdot{}^{t}\Lambda}\mathcal{D}_{X}^{p}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{X}^{q}\xrightarrow{\pi}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\to 0.

iii)

If we put
W^{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}=\pi\big((V^{k}\mathcal{D}_{X})^{p}\oplus(V^{k+1}\mathcal{D}_{X})^{q}\big)
for all
k\in{\mathbf{Z}}
, then we have an induced exact sequence

0\to{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{p}\oplus{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k+1}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{q}\xrightarrow{\cdot{}^{t}\Lambda}{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{p}\oplus{\rm Gr}_{V}^{k+1}(\mathcal{D}_{X})^{q}\xrightarrow{\pi}W^{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}/W^{k+1}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\to 0.

iv)

The polynomial
p(s)=(s+1)b(-s-1)
satisfies
p(s+k)\cdot W^{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\subseteq W^{k+1}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}

for all
k\in{\mathbf{Z}}
. Therefore
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
has a
V
-filtration with respect to
t
, with

W^{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}=V^{>k}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
for all
k\in\mathbf{Z}
, and we have an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]
-modules

(52)

V^{>k}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}/V^{>k+1}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\left(\frac{\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]}{(s+k+1)^{m}\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]}\right)^{p}\oplus\left(\frac{\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]}{(s+k+1)b(-s-k-1)\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]}\right)^{q}.

Proof.

We compute
\mathcal{E}^{*}
using the free resolution in Lemma
7.4
. Applying

\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(-,\mathcal{D}_{X})
to this resolution and passing to left
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules, we see that
\mathcal{E}^{*}
is given by the complex

\mathcal{D}_{X}^{\oplus p}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{X}^{\oplus q}\xrightarrow{\cdot{}^{t}\Lambda}\mathcal{D}_{X}^{\oplus p}\oplus\mathcal{D}_{X}^{\oplus q},

placed in degrees
-n-1
and
-n
, where

{}^{t}\Lambda=\begin{pmatrix}(-s-1)^{m}\text{Id}_{p}&0\\
-{}^{t}Q&-sb(-s)\text{Id}_{q}-{}^{t}P\end{pmatrix}.

Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
7.4
, we see that the map
\cdot{}^{t}\Lambda
is injective, giving the assertions in i) and ii),
and that it induces the exact sequence in iii). The assertion about
p(s)
follows easily and we deduce the existence of the
V
-filtration and the relation
with
W^{\bullet}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
from Remark
5.14
.
∎

We next explain how to relate the
V
-filtrations on
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(b,P,Q)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(b,P,Q)
.
For every
\lambda\in{\mathbf{Q}}
and every positive integer
d
, we consider the
k
-bilinear map

k[s]/(s-\lambda)^{d}\times k[s]/(s-\lambda)^{d}\to k

that maps
\big((s-\lambda)^{i},(s-\lambda)^{j}\big)
to
1
if
i+j=d-1
and to
0
, otherwise. We get in this way a
k
-linear isomorphism

(53)

k[s]/(s-\lambda)^{d}\to{\rm Hom}_{k}\big(k[s]/(s-\lambda)^{d},k\big).

Note now that we have an isomorphism

(54)

\mathcal{H}^{-n}\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s-\lambda)^{d})^{*}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{H}^{\ell}\otimes_{k}{\rm Hom}_{k}\big(k[s]/(s-\lambda)^{d},k\big),

where
\mathcal{D}_{H}^{\ell}
is the left
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module corresponding to the standard right
\mathcal{D}_{H}
-module structure on
\mathcal{D}_{H}
, and we thus get an isomorphism

(55)

{\rm nat}_{\lambda,d}\colon\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s-\lambda)^{d}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{D}_{H}^{\ell}[s]/(s-\lambda)^{d}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{H}^{-n}\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s-\lambda)^{d})^{*},

where the first isomorphism is induced by applying the classical adjoint in
\mathcal{D}_{H}
to the coefficients
and the second one is obtained by tensoring (
53
) with
\mathcal{D}_{X}^{\ell}

and using (
54
).

Let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(b,P,Q)
be an elementary module and
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\mathcal{H}^{-n}(\mathcal{E}^{*})
the corresponding

\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module described in Lemma
7.5
.
We first relate
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1-\lambda}(\mathcal{E})
and
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\lambda}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})
for
\lambda\in(0,1)
.
Let
m_{\lambda}
be the multiplicity of
\lambda-1
as a root of
b(s)
.
Since

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1-\lambda}(\mathcal{E})\simeq\big\{u\in V^{0}\mathcal{E}/V^{1}\mathcal{E}\mid(s+1-\lambda)^{m_{\lambda}}u=0\big\},

it follows from (
50
) that we have an isomorphism

(56)

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1-\lambda}(\mathcal{E})\simeq\big\{u\in(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m})^{p}\oplus(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/sb(s))^{q}\mid(s+1-\lambda)^{m_{\lambda}}u=0\}\simeq\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1-\lambda)^{m_{\lambda}}\big)^{q}.

Similarly, using Lemma
7.5
, we get

{\rm Gr}^{\lambda}_{V}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\simeq\big\{u\in V^{>0}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}/V^{>1}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}=W^{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}/W^{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\mid(s+\lambda)^{m_{\lambda}}u=0\big\}

hence (
52
) gives an isomorphism

(57)

{\rm Gr}^{\lambda}_{V}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\simeq\big\{u\in(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m})^{p}\oplus(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)b(-s-1))^{q}\mid(s+\lambda)^{m_{\lambda}}=0\big\}

\simeq\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+\lambda)^{m_{\lambda}}\big).

It follows from (
56
) that
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1-\lambda}(\mathcal{E})
is a free
\mathcal{D}_{H}
-module, hence
\mathcal{H}^{i}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{1-\lambda}(\mathcal{E})^{*}\big)=0
for all
i\neq-n
and
we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{H}^{-n}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{1-\lambda}(\mathcal{E})^{*}\big)\simeq\mathcal{H}^{-n}\left(\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1-\lambda)^{m_{\lambda}}\big)^{*}\right)^{q}\simeq\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1-\lambda)^{m_{\lambda}}\big)^{q},

where the second isomorphism is provided by
{\rm nat}_{\lambda-1,m_{\lambda}}
.
Using (
57
) and mapping
s
to
-s-1
, we get an isomorphism

\delta_{\lambda}\colon{\rm Gr}^{\lambda}_{V}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{H}^{-n}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{1-\lambda}(\mathcal{E})^{*}\big),

that satisfies
\delta_{\lambda}\circ(s+\lambda)=(-s-1+\lambda)\circ\delta_{\lambda}
.

We proceed similarly to relate
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\mathcal{E})
and
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})
. Recall that
m
is the multiplicity of
0
as a root of
b(s)
, hence
we have an isomorphism

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\mathcal{E})\simeq\big\{u\in V^{0}\mathcal{E}/V^{1}\mathcal{E}\mid s^{m+1}u=0\big\}

and using (
50
), we obtain an isomorphism

(58)

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\mathcal{E})\simeq\big\{u\in(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m})^{p}\oplus(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/sb(s))^{q}\mid s^{m+1}u=0\big\}\simeq\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m}\big)^{p}\oplus\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m+1}\big)^{q}.

Similarly, we have an isomorphism

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\simeq\big\{u\in V^{>-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}/V^{>0}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}=W^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}/W^{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\mid s^{m+1}u=0\big\}

and using the isomorphism (
52
), we obtain an isomorphism

(59)

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\simeq\big\{u\in(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m})^{p}\oplus(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/sb(-s))^{q}\mid s^{m+1}u=0\big\}

\simeq\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m}\big)^{p}\oplus\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m+1}\big)^{q}.

It follows from (
58
) that
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\mathcal{E})
is a free
\mathcal{D}_{H}
-module, hence
\mathcal{H}^{i}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\mathcal{E})\big)=0
for all
i\neq-n
and
we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{H}^{-n}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\mathcal{E})^{*}\big)\simeq\mathcal{H}^{-n}\left(\big((\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m})^{*}\big)^{p}\oplus\big((\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m+1})^{*}\big)\right)^{q}\simeq\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m}\big)^{p}\oplus\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/s^{m+1}\big)^{q},

where the second isomorphism is provided by
{\rm nat}_{0,m}
and
{\rm nat}_{0,m+1}
. Using (
59
) and mapping
s
to
-s
, we get an isomorphism

\delta_{0}\colon{\rm Gr}^{0}_{V}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{H}^{-n}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{0}(\mathcal{E})^{*}\big),

such that
\delta_{0}\circ s=(-s)\circ\delta_{0}
.

Finally, we relate
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\mathcal{E})
and
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})
. We have an isomorphism

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\mathcal{E})\simeq\big\{u\in V^{1}\mathcal{E}/V^{2}\mathcal{E}\mid(s+1)^{m+1}u=0\}

and using (
50
), we obtain an isomorphism

(60)

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\mathcal{E})\simeq\big\{u\in(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m})^{p}\oplus(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)b(s+1))^{q}\mid(s+1)^{m+1}u=0\big\}

\simeq\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m}\big)^{p}\oplus\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m+1}\big)^{q}.

Similarly, we have an isomorphism

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\simeq\big\{u\in V^{>0}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}/V^{>1}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}=W^{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}/W^{1}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\mid(s+1)^{m+1}u=0\big\},

hence using the isomorphism (
52
), we obtain an isomorphism

(61)

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\simeq\big\{u\in(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m})^{p}\oplus(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)b(-s-1))^{q}\mid(s+1)^{m+1}u=0\big\}

\simeq\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m}\big)^{p}\oplus\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m+1}\big)^{q}.

It follows from the isomorphism (
60
) that
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\mathcal{E})
is a free
\mathcal{D}_{H}
-module, hence
\mathcal{H}^{i}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\mathcal{E})\big)=0
for all
i\neq-n
and
we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{H}^{-n}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\mathcal{E})^{*}\big)\simeq\mathcal{H}^{-n}\left(\big((\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m})^{*}\big)^{p}\oplus\big((\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m+1})^{*}\big)^{q}\right)

\simeq\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m}\big)^{p}\oplus\big(\mathcal{D}_{H}[s]/(s+1)^{m+1}\big)^{q},

where the second isomorphism is provided by
{\rm nat}_{-1,m}
and
{\rm nat}_{-1,m+1}
.
Using (
61
) and mapping
s
to
-s-2
, we get an isomorphism

\delta_{1}\colon{\rm Gr}^{1}_{V}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{H}^{-n}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{1}(\mathcal{E})^{*}\big),

such that
\delta_{1}\circ s=(-s-2)\circ\delta_{0}
.

We can now address the compatibility of
V
-filtrations with duality:

Sketch of proof of Theorem
7.1
.

We have constructed the isomorphisms
\delta_{\alpha}
in Theorem
7.1
, for
\alpha\in[0,1]
, when
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{E}(b,P,Q)

and we have a system of coordinates
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},t
on
X
. It is an important fact that these isomorphisms are functorial with
respect to morphisms between such modules, but we do not discuss this here and refer instead to
[
MaisonobeMebkhout
, pp. 356, 359, 361]
.
Using this, we can now extend the constructions of the isomorphisms
\delta_{\alpha}
to an arbitrary coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
on
X

that admits a
V
-filtration,
under the further assumption that
X
is affine. Indeed, by a repeated application of Lemma
7.3
, we have a resolution
by elementary
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules

\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}\to\mathcal{M}\to 0
, such that
\mathcal{E}^{i}=\mathcal{E}(b_{i},P_{i},Q_{i})
for
i\leq 0
. It follows that via the left-right equivalence of categories,

\mathcal{M}^{*}
corresponds to
{\mathbf{R}}\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet},\mathcal{D}_{X})[n+1]
.

By Lemma
7.5
, we know that for all
i\in{\mathbf{Z}}
, we have
\mathcal{E}xt^{j}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(\mathcal{E}^{i},\mathcal{D}_{X})=0
for
j\neq 1

and the left
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module corresponding to
\mathcal{E}xt^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(\mathcal{E}^{i},\mathcal{D}_{X})
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}
. We deduce that

\mathcal{M}^{*}\simeq\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\bullet}[n]
and using the exactness of
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}(-)
, we conclude that

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}\big(\mathcal{H}^{j}(\mathcal{M}^{*})\big)\simeq\mathcal{H}^{j+n}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\bullet})\big).

On the other hand, for every
\alpha
, we have seen that each
{\rm Gr}^{\alpha}_{V}(\mathcal{E}^{i})
is a free
\mathcal{D}_{H}
-module,
hence by exactness of
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}(-)
, we get a resolution by free
\mathcal{D}_{H}
-modules

{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet})\to{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}\to 0.

It follows that via the left-right equivalence of categories, the dual

\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}\big)^{*}
corresponds to
\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{D}_{H}}\big({\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}),\mathcal{D}_{H}\big)[n]
, and thus

\mathcal{H}^{j}\big({\rm Gr}^{\alpha}_{V}(\mathcal{M})^{*}\big)\simeq\mathcal{H}^{j+n}\left(\mathcal{H}^{-n}\big({\rm Gr}^{\alpha}_{V}(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet})^{*}\big)\right).

The functoriality of the isomorphisms
\delta_{\lambda}
,
\delta_{0}
, and
\delta_{1}
for the
\mathcal{E}^{i}
implies that we get
corresponding isomorphisms for
\mathcal{M}
.

Given any two resolutions
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\bullet}\to\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\bullet}\to\mathcal{M}
by elementary
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules, there is a third one

\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\bullet}\to\mathcal{M}
which dominates those two. Using again the functoriality of the isomorphisms
\delta_{\alpha}
for elementary modules,
one can check that all three resolutions induce the same isomorphisms for
\mathcal{M}
. This implies that for arbitrary
X
, we can construct the isomorphisms

\delta_{\alpha}
by gluing the corresponding isomorphisms on a suitable affine open cover. Furthermore, one can prove the functoriality of the
\delta_{\alpha}

using again the special case of functoriality for morphisms between elementary
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules. Finally, the assertions iv), v), and vi) in the theorem can be easily checked by checking them for elementary
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules. This concludes our sketch of the proof of Theorem
7.1
.
∎

We have the following useful corollary of Theorem
7.1
:

Corollary 7.6
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module which admits a
V
-filtration along
H
. Then for all
\alpha\in\mathbf{Q}
, the
\mathcal{D}_{H}
-module
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}
is holonomic.

Proof.

The assertion follows, using the theorem, from the fact that a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
is holonomic if and only if
\mathcal{H}^{j}(\mathcal{M}^{*})=0
for all
j\neq 0
, see

[
HTT
, Cor. 2.6.8]
.
∎

Remark 7.7

(The case of regular holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules)
.

In fact, if
\mathcal{M}
is a regular holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module, then
{\rm Gr}_{V}^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}
is also regular holonomic for all
\alpha\in\mathbf{Q}
, see

[
MaisonobeMebkhout
, Cor. 4.7-5]
, as well as Corollary
9.10
below.

Remark 7.8

(Compatibility with duality for
V
-filtrations with respect to arbitrary hypersurfaces)
.

We have only stated Theorem
7.1
in the case of a smooth hypersurface. However, given any nonzero
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
, if
\iota\colon X\hookrightarrow X\times{\mathbf{A}}^{1}
is the corresponding graph embedding, then we have
\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})^{*}\simeq\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M}^{*})
by
[
HTT
, Section 2.7.2]
.
It follows that by applying Theorem
7.1
for
\iota_{+}(\mathcal{M})
, we obtain a corresponding statement for the
V
-filtrations with respect to
f
.

8.
Push-forward and
V
-filtrations

We turn to the behavior of
V
-filtrations under proper push-forward. Let
\pi\colon X\to Y
be a morphism between smooth algebraic varieties and let
\mathcal{M}
be a coherent

\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module such that
\pi
is proper on the support of
\mathcal{M}
. Note that in this case

\mathcal{H}^{j}(\pi_{+}\mathcal{M})
is a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{Y}
-module for all
j
(see
[
Sabbah2
, Theorem 3.4.1]
).
Suppose that
f
is a nonzero regular function on
Y
. We are interested in the relation between the
V
-filtration on
\mathcal{M}
with respect to
f\circ\pi
and the
V
-filtration on

\mathcal{H}^{j}(\pi_{+}\mathcal{M})
with respect to
f
.

8.1.
The case of smooth projections

We first consider the situation of a smooth projection
p\colon X=Y\times Z\to Y
, proper on the support of
\mathcal{M}
, and of a nonzero
t\in\mathcal{O}_{Y}(Y)
that defines a smooth hypersurface. The general situation will be reduced to this case.
With a slight abuse of notation, we also denote by
t
the pull-back
t\circ p
. We put
d=\dim(Z)
.

Recall that by formula (
5
), we have

\mathcal{H}^{j}(p_{+}\mathcal{M})=R^{j}p_{*}\big(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}[d]\big).

Note that since
X=Y\times Z
, we have
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}=V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{Y}\boxtimes\mathcal{D}_{Z}
, hence for every
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{T}
, we can form
the relative de Rham complex

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{T}
.
In particular,
if
\mathcal{M}
has a
V
-filtration
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
with respect to
t
,
then for every
j\in{\mathbf{Z}}
and every
\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}}
, we may consider

V^{\alpha}\mathcal{H}^{j}(p_{+}\mathcal{M}):={\rm Im}\left(R^{j}p_{*}\big(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}V^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}[d]\big)\to R^{j}p_{*}\big(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}[d]\big)\right).

It is clear that this is a
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{Y}
-submodule of
\mathcal{H}^{j}(p_{+}\mathcal{M})
.

Theorem 8.1
.

With the above notation, if
\mathcal{M}
is a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module such that
p
is proper on
\mathrm{Supp\,}(\mathcal{M})

and
\mathcal{M}
has a
V
-filtration
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{M}
with respect to
t
, then for every
j\in{\mathbf{Z}}
and
\alpha\in{\mathbf{Q}}
, the canonical morphism

(62)

R^{j}p_{*}\big(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}V^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}[d]\big)\to R^{j}p_{*}\big(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}[d]\big)

is injective. Moreover,
V^{\bullet}\mathcal{H}^{j}(p_{+}\mathcal{M})

is the
V
-filtration of
\mathcal{H}^{j}(p_{+}\mathcal{M})
with respect to
t
.

Proof.

We give the proof in
4
steps, adapting an approach due to Sabbah, see
[
MebkhoutSabbah
, Section III.4.8]
.

Step 1
. We prove that for every locally finitely generated
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
{\mathcal{T}}
such that
p
is proper on
{\rm Supp}(\mathcal{T})

and every
j\in{\mathbf{Z}}
, the
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{Y}
-module
R^{j}p_{*}\big(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{T}[d]\big)
is locally finitely generated
(in particular this will be the case for
\mathcal{T}=V^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}
).
Since
\mathcal{T}
is locally finitely generated over
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
, we can find a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathcal{T}

such that the induced map

V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{T}

in surjective. Note that the kernel is again locally finitely generated over
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}
.
In order to show, by descending induction on
j
, that
R^{j}p_{*}\big(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{T}[d]\big)
is locally finitely generated over
V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{Y}
for all

\mathcal{T}
as above, it is thus enough, by a standard cohomological argument, to see that this is the case when
\mathcal{T}=V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{F}
,
for a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{F}
such that
p
is proper on
{\rm Supp}(\mathcal{F})
.
Since

V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{X}=V^{0}\mathcal{D}_{Y}\boxtimes\mathcal{D}_{Z}
